## ![](_page_0_Picture_2.jpeg)

# **March for Our Lives**

**March for Our Lives** (**MFOL**) is a [student-led](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Student_activism) organization which leads [demonstrations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demonstration_(protest)) in support of [gun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_control) [control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_control) legislation.[\[4\]](#page-16-1) The first demonstration took place in [Washington, D. C.,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington,_D. C.) on March 24, 2018, with over 880 sibling events throughout the United States and around the world,[\[5\]](#page-16-2)[\[6\]](#page-17-0)[\[7\]](#page-17-1)[\[8\]](#page-17-2)[\[9\]](#page-17-3) and was planned by [Never Again MSD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Never_Again_MSD) in collaboration with the nonprofi[t organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nonprofit_organization) [Everytown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Everytown_for_Gun_Safety) [for Gun Safety.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Everytown_for_Gun_Safety)[\[10\]](#page-17-4) The event followed the [Parkland high](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parkland_high_school_shooting) [school shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parkland_high_school_shooting) a month earlier, which was described by several media outlets as a possible [tipping point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tipping_point_(sociology)) for [gun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_control) [control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_control) legislation[.\[11\]](#page-17-5)[\[12\]](#page-17-6)[\[13\]](#page-17-7)

Protesters urged for [universal background checks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_background_check) on all [gun sales,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_sale) closing of the [gun show loophole,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_show_loophole) a restoration of the [1994 Federal Assault Weapons Ban,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Assault_Weapons_Ban) and a [ban on](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-capacity_magazine_ban) [the sale of high-capacity magazines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-capacity_magazine_ban) and [bump stocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bump_stock) in the United States[.\[14\]](#page-17-8) Turnout was estimated to be between 1.2 and 2 million people in the United States[,\[15\]](#page-17-9)[\[16\]](#page-17-10)[\[17\]](#page-17-11) making it one of the [largest protests in American history.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_protests_in_the_United_States_by_size) [\[2\]](#page-16-3)

After the [Robb Elementary School Shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robb_Elementary_School_shooting) in [Uvalde,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uvalde,_Texas) [Texas,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uvalde,_Texas) [MFOL Action Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_for_Our_Lives_Action_Fund) organized another nationwide protest on June 11, 2022[.\[18\]](#page-18-0) The main protest took place in [Washington, D. C.,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington,_D. C.) with hundreds of sibling events taking place across the United States[.\[19\]](#page-18-1)

### **Planning**

[Cameron Kasky,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Kasky) a [junior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_(education_year)) at [Marjory Stoneman Douglas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marjory_Stoneman_Douglas_High_School) [High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marjory_Stoneman_Douglas_High_School) in [Parkland, Florida,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parkland,_Florida) and his classmates, announced the march on February 18, four days after the [shooting at the school.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stoneman_Douglas_High_School_shooting)[\[20\]](#page-18-2) Also joining the march efforts were [Alex Wind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Wind) of Stoneman Douglas High School, who along with four friends created the ["Never Again"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Never_Again_MSD) [campaign.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Never_Again_MSD)[\[10\]](#page-17-4) [X González](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_Gonz%C3%A1lez) and [David Hogg,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Hogg) also survivors of the shooting, have been vocal supporters of the march. [\[21\]](#page-18-3)

#### **March for Our Lives**

Part of [Gun politics in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_politics_in_the_United_States) and [Protests against Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protests_against_Donald_Trump)

The date was chosen in order to give students, families and others a chance to mourn first, and then on March 24, talk about [gun control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_control)[.\[21\]](#page-18-3) Organizers filed a permit application with the [National Park Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Park_Service) during the week of February 23, and expected as many as 500,000 people to attend.[\[22\]](#page-18-4)[\[23\]](#page-18-5) However, the [National Mall,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Mall) which was the

1.2 million people in the United States, and more around the world.[\[2\]](#page-16-3)[\[3\]](#page-16-4) **Website** [marchforourlives.org \(http](https://marchforourlives.org/) [s://marchforourlives.org/\)](https://marchforourlives.org/)

Crowd on [Pennsylvania Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Avenue) (Washington, D. C.) [Cameron Kasky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Kasky) at a rally in February planned site of the main march in [Washington, D. C.,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington,_D. C.)

was reportedly already booked for March 24; the application, filed by an unidentified local student group, claimed it was for a talent show[.\[24\]](#page-18-6) [\[25\]](#page-18-7) A permit was later obtained for [Pennsylvania Avenue.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Avenue)[\[26\]](#page-19-0) The [Washington Metropolitan Area Transit Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Metropolitan_Area_Transit_Authority) announced it would operate extra trains for the march[.\[27\]](#page-19-1)

#### **External videos**

[March For Our Lives Rally,](https://www.c-span.org/video/?442255-1/march-lives-rally-coverage) [Washington, D. C., March 24,](https://www.c-span.org/video/?442255-1/march-lives-rally-coverage) [2018 \(https://www.c-span.or](https://www.c-span.org/video/?442255-1/march-lives-rally-coverage) [g/video/?442255-1/march-liv](https://www.c-span.org/video/?442255-1/march-lives-rally-coverage) [es-rally-coverage\),](https://www.c-span.org/video/?442255-1/march-lives-rally-coverage) [C-SPAN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-SPAN)

The [Enough! National School Walkout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enough!_National_School_Walkout) was held on the one month anniversary of the Stoneman Douglas shooting.[\[28\]](#page-19-2)[\[29\]](#page-19-3) It involved students walking out from their classes for exactly 17 minutes (one for each of the victims of the massacre[\)\[30\]](#page-19-4) and involved more than 3,000 schools across the United State[s\[31\]](#page-19-5)[\[32\]](#page-19-6) and nearly one million students[.\[33\]](#page-19-7) Thousands of students also gathered and staged a rally in Washington, D. C., after observing 17 minutes of silence with their backs to the [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House) [House.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House)[\[29\]](#page-19-3)[\[34\]](#page-20-0) After the success of the walkout, Hogg posted a twee[t\[35\]](#page-20-1) that included a provocative, [NRA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rifle_Association)[style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rifle_Association) advertisement calling out lawmakers for their inaction on or opposition to gun control efforts, asking "What if our politicians weren't the bitch of the NRA?", and ending with a promotion for the upcoming March.[\[36\]](#page-20-2)

### **Celebrity and corporate support**

[George Clooney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Clooney) and [Scooter Braun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scooter_Braun) were major forces behind the organization of the march, and aided in fundraising efforts behind the scenes[.\[37\]](#page-20-3) [Amal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amal_Clooney) and [George Clooney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Clooney) donated \$500,000 to support the march and announced they would attend. [Oprah Winfrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oprah_Winfrey) matched the Clooney donation to support the march[.\[38\]](#page-20-4) [\[39\]](#page-20-5) Jeff[rey Katzenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Katzenberg) and his wife Marilyn also contributed \$500,000.[\[40\]](#page-20-6) Film director and producer [Steven Spielberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Spielberg) and actress [Kate Capshaw Spielberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate_Capshaw) donated \$500,000, also matching the donation of the

Clooneys.[\[41\]](#page-20-7) On February 23, [Gucci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gucci) announced they were also donating \$500,000 towards the march[.\[42\]](#page-21-0) Other people and [John Cena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Cena) and [Millie Bobby Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millie_Bobby_Brown) applauded the March for Our Lives event at the [Kids Choice Awards.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Kids%27_Choice_Awards)[\[58\]](#page-22-7)

The founding members of MFOL were awarded *[Smithsonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smithsonian_Magazine)* [magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smithsonian_Magazine)'s 2018 American Ingenuity Award in the Youth category.[\[59\]](#page-22-8)

In Washington, D. C., a prayer and vigil was held at the [Washington National Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_National_Cathedral) on the eve of the rally, as a memorial for the victims of gun violence, and to declare the church's belief, "This work is rooted in our commitment to Jesus' command to love our neighbors as ourselves... We gather out of a conviction that the right to bear arms does not trump the right to life.["\[60\]](#page-22-9)[\[61\]](#page-22-10)[\[62\]](#page-22-11) The litany also included the following refrain:

From so many heartbreaks comes forth a united commitment to go into the streets of our cities and towns and promote a way of peace and well-being for all people. With compassion sown from the threads of sadness and terror, we will mend a nation tattered by gun violence and weave a new cloth of hope and peace. [\[63\]](#page-23-0)

Prayer and vigil at the [Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_National_Cathedral) [National Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_National_Cathedral)

Guest speakers included Philip and April Schentrup, parents of 16-year-old Carmen Schentrup, who was killed in the [shooting in Parkland, Florida.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stoneman_Douglas_High_School_shooting)[\[60\]](#page-22-9)[\[63\]](#page-23-0)

### **Participation**

March for Our Lives was among the biggest youth-led protests since the [Vietnam War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_in_the_Vietnam_War) era.[\[64\]](#page-23-1)[\[65\]](#page-23-2)[\[66\]](#page-23-3) Estimates of participation at the main event in Washington, D. C., range from 200,000 to 800,000.[\[67\]](#page-23-4)[\[68\]](#page-23-5)[\[69\]](#page-23-6)

The speakers—all of whom were high schoolers or younger included Marjory Stoneman Douglas students [Cameron Kasky,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Kasky) [David Hogg,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Hogg) [Delaney Tarr,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Never_Again_MSD) [Sarah Chadwick,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Chadwick_(activist)) [Alex Wind,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Wind) [Jaclyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaclyn_Corin) [Corin,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaclyn_Corin) [Ryan Deitsch,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryan_Deitsch) [Aalayah Eastmond,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aalayah_Eastmond) Samantha Fuentes, and [X González](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_Gonz%C3%A1lez)[.\[70\]](#page-23-7)[\[71\]](#page-23-8)[\[72\]](#page-23-9)[\[69\]](#page-23-6)[\[73\]](#page-23-10) [Hunter Pollack,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter_Pollack) brother of victim [Meadow,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meadow_Pollack) was scheduled to speak, but did not attend due to a logistical issue[,\[74\]](#page-23-11) which he contended was a result of being misled by event officials[.\[75\]](#page-23-12)[\[76\]](#page-24-0) David Hogg tweeted out a video of Hunter's speech from a later event.[\[77\]](#page-24-1)

## ![](_page_3_Picture_3.jpeg)

Portion of speech by [David Hogg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Hogg)

Other participants included Naomi Wadler, who is an elementary school student in [Alexandria, Virginia,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria,_Virginia)[\[78\]](#page-24-2) [\[79\]](#page-24-3)[\[80\]](#page-24-4) Trevon Bosley from Chicago whose brother was shot and killed leaving church[,\[81\]](#page-24-5) Edna Lizbeth Chávez, a high school student from Los Angeles,[\[82\]](#page-24-6) and Zion Kelly, whose twin brother was shot and killed during an armed robbery.[\[83\]](#page-24-7) [Yolanda Renee King,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yolanda_Renee_King) granddaughter of [Martin Luther King Jr.,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Luther_King_Jr.) also made an appearanc[e\[84\]](#page-24-8) along with Mya Middleton, a student from Chicago representing [After School Matters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/After_School_Matters)[,\[85\]](#page-24-9) Matt Post, a senior from Montgomery County,[\[86\]](#page-24-10) Christopher Underwood, an 11-year old from New York, [\[87\]](#page-24-11) Alex King and D'Angelo Mc Dade from Chicago[,\[88\]](#page-25-0) and Matthew Soto, brother of [Sandy Hook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandy_Hook_Elementary_School_shooting) victim [Victoria Soto,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Leigh_Soto)[\[89\]](#page-25-1) and Parkland parents Lori Alhadeff, who lost her daughter Alyssa in the school incident[\[90\]](#page-25-2) and Manuel & Patricia Oliver, whose lost their son Joaquin and launched a campaign titled "Change The Ref" to honor their son and push for new restrictions on any form of weapon violence.[\[91\]](#page-25-3)

González, after speaking and naming the seventeen victims, stood silent for over four minutes, after which a cellphone alarm went off and they announced the six minute and twenty second point in their speech, equal to the length of the Parkland shooting.[\[92\]](#page-25-4)[\[93\]](#page-25-5)[\[94\]](#page-25-6) González ended their speech saying,

Since the time that I came out here, it has been 6 minutes and 20 seconds. The shooter has ceased shooting, and will soon abandon his rifle, blend in with the students as they escape, and walk free for an hour before arrest. Fight for your lives before it's someone else's job.[\[95\]](#page-25-7)

Their speech and emotional [moment of silence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moment_of_silence) was praised by media organizations as one of the "most memorable["\[96\]](#page-25-8) and "powerful" moments in the day's events.[\[97\]](#page-25-9)[\[98\]](#page-25-10)

Celebs who took part in the rally in Los Angeles included [Laura Dern,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Dern) [Reese Witherspoon,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reese_Witherspoon) [Olivia Wilde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivia_Wilde) with [Jason Sudeikis,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Sudeikis) [Mason Cook,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mason_Cook) [Jaden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaden_Smith) & [Willow Smith,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willow_Smith) [Yara Shahidi,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yara_Shahidi) [Meg Donnelly,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meg_Donnelly) Roots Drummer [Questlove,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Questlove) [Amy Schumer,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amy_Schumer) [Lady Gaga,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_Gaga) [Charlie Puth,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Puth) [Mae Whitman,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mae_Whitman) [Connie Britton,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connie_Britton) [Rita Ora,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rita_Ora) [Ta'Rhonda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ta%27Rhonda_Jones) [Jones,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ta%27Rhonda_Jones) [Miles Heizer,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miles_Heizer) [Kendall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kendall_Jenner) & [Kylie Jenner,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kylie_Jenner) [Hailey Baldwin,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hailey_Baldwin) [Leona Lewis,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leona_Lewis) [Anjelica Huston,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anjelica_Huston) and singer and music executive [Diane Warren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diane_Warren) along with city's mayor [Eric Garcetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Garcetti) and Senator [Kamala Harris.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris)

During the rally in Miami Beach, musicians [Flo Rida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flo_Rida) and Gloria & Emilio Estefan's daughter [Emily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emily_Estefan) took part in a Miami Beach rally with Mayor [Dan Gelber.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Gelber)

Actor [Matthew Mc Conaughey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_Mc Conaughey) took part in the march in Austin, Texas, and other singer-songwriters included [Paul Simon,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Simon) who performed ["Sound of Silence"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sound_of_Silence) in Stamford, Connecticut, and [Brandi Carlile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandi_Carlile) who sung ["Hold Out Your Hand"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hold_Out_Your_Hand), following the release of her sixth studio album ["By the Way, I Forgive You"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/By_the_Way,_I_Forgive_You), which was released two days after the Parkland tragedy. (The song later became a music video on June 1, 2018, showing montage recap clips of protestors being guided by officers on motorcycle while Brandi is blending in with the crowd and is later seen performing on the front lawn of the [Seattle Center.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Center))

Politicians included [Andrew Yang,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Yang) who took part in Annapolis along with Mayor [Gavin Buckley,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gavin_Buckley) Maryland, Nashville Mayor [David Briley,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Briley) Washington, D. C. Mayor [Muriel Bowser,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muriel_Bowser) Georgia Congressman [John Lewis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Lewis) (who took part in [Atlanta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlanta) and paid tribute for his colleague [Dr. Martin Luther King Jr.,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr._Martin_Luther_King_Jr.) [John Fitzgerald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_F._Kennedy) and [Robert Francis Kennedy,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Francis_Kennedy) all of whom died from weapon violence), Representative [Joseph Kennedy III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Kennedy_III) in the event in [Boston,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston) Massachusetts Senator [Elizabeth Warren,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Warren) who snapped a few selfies during a march in [Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Massachusetts)field, and New York Governor [Andrew Cuomo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Cuomo) during the New York City event along with Mayor [Bill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_De_Blasio) [De Blasio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_De_Blasio) and [Black Lives Matter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Lives_Matter) President, [Hawk Newsome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawk_Newsome) as well as TV Host [Nick Cannon.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Cannon)

Other advocates/activists included [Malala Yousafzai,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malala_Yousafzai) who gave a warm introduction via a video monitor during the Washington March.

### **Responses**

#### **National Rifle Association**

On March 21, [NRATV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NRATV) host Grant Stinchfield stated that "March for Our Lives is backed by radicals with a history of violent threats, language and actions"; fact-checker [Politi Fact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politi Fact) has rated this statement as being "without merit" and ["Pants on Fire"](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/liar_liar_pants_on_fire) indicating that it is a "ridiculous claim".[\[100\]](#page-26-0)[\[101\]](#page-26-1)

While the march was occurring, the [NRA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rifle_Association) posted a membership drive video on their Facebook page, declaring that the "protests aren't spontaneous. Gun-hating billionaires and Hollywood elites are manipulating and exploiting children as part of their plan to DESTROY the [Second Amendment"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Amendment_to_the_United_States_Constitution). Another video dubbed "A March for Their Lies" was uploaded to [You Tube](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You Tube) featuring [Colion Noir,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colion_Noir) in which he described the planned rally as a "carnival of a march". Noir also said in the video that there is an "agenda that's a million times bigger than the guns"[.\[102\]](#page-26-2)[\[103\]](#page-26-3)

#### **Politicians**

*[The Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Washington_Post)* reported that there were many [Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_party_of_the_United_States) encouraging the marchers, and many of them, including candidates for office, participated from the sidelines in the march, but few [Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_party_of_the_United_States) did similarly.[\[104\]](#page-26-4) The White House said in a response that they "applaud the many courageous young Americans exercising their fi[rst amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Amendment_to_the_United_States_Constitution) rights."[\[105\]](#page-26-5)

On the day of the protests, Florida [Republican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_party_of_the_United_States) senator [Marco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Rubio) [Rubio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Rubio) responded by stating: "However, many other Americans do not support a gun ban" and "view banning guns as an infringement on the Second Amendment rights of law abiding citizens that ultimately will not prevent these tragedies." He called for protesters to find "common ground with those who hold opposing views" for change to happen. However, a blanket gun ban was not called for by the protests.[\[106\]](#page-26-6)[\[107\]](#page-26-7)[\[108\]](#page-26-8)

Former Republican senator and presidential candidate [Rick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Santorum) [Santorum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Santorum) criticized the Parkland activists, suggesting during an interview with [CNN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNN) that students should be learning ways to respond to a shooter rather than asking lawmakers "to solve their problem"; Santorum advised students to take classes in [CPR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiopulmonary_resuscitation) rather than marching in Washington.[\[109\]](#page-26-9)[\[110\]](#page-26-10) *[The Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Washington_Post) [Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Washington_Post)* quoted several doctors responding to Santorum that CPR would not be at all effective on gunshot victims as they were suffering from blood loss[.\[111\]](#page-27-0)

A poster saying "Our children's safety should not have to be leveraged"

## ![](_page_5_Picture_9.jpeg)

The [Governor of New York,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_New_York) [Andrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Cuomo) [Cuomo,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Cuomo) leading the NYC March For Our Lives rally

### **Media**

A report in *[The New Yorker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Yorker)* praised the leaders of the march for their "extraordinary inclusiveness" in that they expanded the locus of concern from suburban schools to those of urban neighborhoods as well[.\[112\]](#page-27-1)

[Libertarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libertarianism_in_the_United_States) magazine *[Reason](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reason_(magazine))* criticized the march, saying that "Gun violence has declined precipitously over the past 25 years, and most Americans are much safer today than they were a generation ago." and that "mass shootings are not the norm, and kids don't need to be terrified of going to school.["\[113\]](#page-27-2)

On social media, fake pictures and [GIFs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GIF) of [X González](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_Gonz%C3%A1lez) tearing up a copy of the [U. S. Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U. S._Constitution) were circulated in an effort to discredit the march. The images were doctored from originals of González tearing up a shooting target sign. Actor and conservative commentator [Adam Baldwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Baldwin) defended circulating the doctored images as "political satire."[\[114\]](#page-27-3)[\[115\]](#page-27-4)

### **Use of social media**

Previously, protests had occurred for multiple [shootings in the United States,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_shootings_in_the_United_States) such as the 2015 [Charleston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charleston_church_shooting) [church shooting,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charleston_church_shooting) but never had they amassed more than several hundred participants. With social media, more information was relayed to a larger audience in quicker time, giving more people awareness of what was happening across the nation.[\[116\]](#page-27-5) By National Walkout Day on April 20, 2018, the social medias had followings over 1.3 million people and in Washington, D. C., alone, 200,000 people attended the March For Our Lives protest, whom many younger adults attributed to the big social media presence.[\[117\]](#page-27-6) The hashtag #March For Our Lives was used 3.6 million times, and over 7.5 thousand tweets were directed at the NRA social media account.

#### **Twitter**

The movement originated after the [Parkland shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stoneman_Douglas_High_School_shooting) in Florida, where 17 individuals died[.\[118\]](#page-27-7) During the shooting, multiple students used [Twitter,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter) including freshman Aidan Minoff, who tweeted, "I am in a school shooting right now..."[\[119\]](#page-27-8) Twitter allowed others around the world to suddenly be aware of what was happening in real time and showing the raw emotion of these students. Twitter supported greater two-way communication between the organization and the audience than traditional news media outlets.

The March For Our Lives Twitter started in February 2018, with the Twitter handle, @AMarch4Our Lives. Up to date, the Twitter account has 450,000 followers and are a student-run organization with a large social media presence[.\[120\]](#page-27-9) The March For Our Lives Twitter has real time, up to date tweets about the movement. It includes tweets about the red flag bill being signed in from other March For Our Lives accounts based in different locations, such as NYC[.\[121\]](#page-27-10) In addition, the March For Our Lives Twitter account also alerts their followers about the events that are happening, such as meetings at Town Halls or when certain protests are happening.[\[122\]](#page-27-11)

#### **Instagram**

In March 2018, [Selena Gomez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selena_Gomez) shared the march's hashtag #March For Our Lives with the message: "Protect kids, not guns!" and this post had received 2 million likes.[\[123\]](#page-28-0) Lady Gaga also documented the march, releasing a series of Instagram videos calling for action from politicians to enforce stricter gun laws[.\[124\]](#page-28-1) The march has drawn support from Taylor Swift, Miley Cyrus, Kim Kardashian, Ariana Grande, and they have pledged to join and perform at the march.[\[125\]](#page-28-2)

The students who survived the Stoneman Douglas High School shooting and millions of students worldwide participated in March for Our Lives, emerging as more influential on Instagram than celebrities on the gun control[.\[126\]](#page-28-3) One of the tools they used were the hashtags. Top hashtags such as #March For Our Lives #Never Again, #Gun Control Now, and #Enough IsEnough are used to spread the word and call on the public's attention.[\[127\]](#page-28-4) Instagram becomes an 'informal' narrative platform that promoted a 'clicktivism' type of responses from the audience for the march.[\[128\]](#page-28-5) Besides, students collaborate with Bartle Bogle Hegarty (BBH) to create the first Instagram coloring book. BBH L. A. executive creative director Zach Hilder said, "we wanted to give them tools to elevate their voices, create a way to unify their message and allow everyone to participate in the march. That's the inspiration for Color for Our Lives."[\[129\]](#page-28-6)

#### **Facebook**

The March For Our Lives movement employs [Facebook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Facebook) as another social media platform to spread awareness of its campaigns and messages to people around the world. As of March 5, 2019, nineteen days before the one year anniversary of the protests, the MFOL Facebook page had garnered more than 300,000 followers[.\[130\]](#page-28-7) The page is mainly used to provide updates on national policies regarding gun laws, as well as coverage of various politicians who advocate for more gun safety. Similar to other media platforms, the Facebook page frequently employs popular hashtags such as #March For Our Lives and #Never Again in its posts. Although other social media platforms such as Twitter and Instagram often make posts to increase awareness, Facebook is more often used to organize people in nearby marches or demonstrations in major cities, and this has seen great success[.\[131\]](#page-28-8) For example, Facebook played a role in the March 24, 2018, March For Our Lives and Never Again collaboration gathering in Washington, D. C., where more than 47,000 people marked themselves as "going," and where more than 90,000 people from across the nation and world marked themselves as "interested" on the Facebook events posting[.\[132\]](#page-28-9)

#### **Snapchat**

The March For Our Lives movement leveraged [Snapchat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snapchat) to gain momentum, spread the word and draw attention to what students and supporters were doing around the United States in response to recent [shootings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_shooting) [on school campuses.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_shooting) Unique from other social media platforms, Snapchat contains a map feature that allowed the world to see when and where activities by its users is taking place. This allowed the student walkouts to be easily tracked around the US.[\[133\]](#page-28-10) Video shows thousands of students and supporters walking the streets, protesting gun violence and current gun laws[.\[134\]](#page-28-11) Snapchat created a "March For Our Lives" Sticker that could be used by the platform's users to document the walkouts around the US.[\[135\]](#page-28-12)

#### **Celebrity controversy**

[Jesse Hughes,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesse_Hughes_(musician)) a survivor of the [Bataclan terrorist attack,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2015_Paris_attacks#Bataclan_theatre_massacre) called the march "pathetic", but after being criticized for his comment, later apologized saying, "I was not attempting to impugn the youth of America and this beautiful thing that they accomplished. I truly am sorry, I did not mean to hurt anyone or cause any harm."[\[136\]](#page-28-13)[\[137\]](#page-28-14)[\[138\]](#page-29-0)[\[139\]](#page-29-1)

## **Locations**

#### **United States**

#### **Maps**

### **Washington, D. C.**

In anticipation and planning of the day's events, many streets in the nation's capital were closed to vehicle traffic.[\[140\]](#page-29-2)[\[141\]](#page-29-3) Several blocks of streets encompassing much of the National Mall, stretching from the [Washington Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Monument) to the [United States Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Capitol) and from [Independence Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Avenue_(Washington,_D. C.)) to E Street, were closed to vehicle traffic[.\[142\]](#page-29-4)[\[143\]](#page-29-5) Some of the rally-goers grouped at [Trump Hotel,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Post_Office_(Washington,_D. C.)#Trump_International_Hotel_Washington,_D. C.) expressing displeasure that the [Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_presidency_of_Donald_Trump) [administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_presidency_of_Donald_Trump) has not addressed school safety nor gun control in a meaningful way[.\[144\]](#page-29-6)

In [Connecticut,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connecticut) marches took place in [Hartford,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hartford,_Connecticut)[\[145\]](#page-29-7)[\[146\]](#page-29-8) [East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Haddam,_Connecticut) [Haddam,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Haddam,_Connecticut)[\[147\]](#page-29-9) Enfi[eld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enfield,_Connecticut)[,\[148\]](#page-29-10) [Guilford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guilford,_Connecticut)[,\[149\]](#page-30-0) [Middlebury,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middlebury,_Connecticut) [New](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Haven,_Connecticut) [Haven,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Haven,_Connecticut) [Old Saybrook,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Saybrook,_Connecticut)[\[150\]](#page-30-1) [Pawcatuck,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pawcatuck,_Connecticut) [Roxbury,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roxbury,_Connecticut) [Salisbury,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salisbury,_Connecticut) [Shelton,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shelton,_Connecticut) [Stamford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stamford,_Connecticut)[\[151\]](#page-30-2) and [Westport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westport,_Connecticut)[.\[152\]](#page-30-3)

In [New Hampshire,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Hampshire) marches took place in [Concord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concord,_New_Hampshire)[\[166\]](#page-31-7) and [Portsmouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portsmouth,_New_Hampshire)[\[167\]](#page-31-8) and [Jackson.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jackson,_New_Hampshire)[\[168\]](#page-31-9) The Concord event was organized by Eve Caplan, a sophomore at [John Stark Regional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Stark_Regional_High_School) [High School,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Stark_Regional_High_School) and another high school student in [Plymouth, New](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plymouth,_New_Hampshire) [Hampshire.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plymouth,_New_Hampshire)[\[166\]](#page-31-7) Portsmouth had a demonstration in Market Square, and was organized Sarah Mae Brown, a leader of The Resistance Seacoast.[\[167\]](#page-31-8)

the event.[\[237\]](#page-38-3) Twenty-one Kansas and Missouri organizations hosted the event[.\[238\]](#page-38-4) The rally culminated in a march through the nearby Country Club Plaza upscale shopping district[.\[239\]](#page-38-5) Organizers partnered with the Poor People's Campaign[\[240\]](#page-38-6) to promote their Faith Assembly at Community Christian Church—planned by Kansas and Missouri organizers together.[\[241\]](#page-38-7)

In [North Dakota,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Dakota) marches took place in [Fargo,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fargo,_North_Dakota)[\[242\]](#page-38-8) [Bismarck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bismarck,_North_Dakota)[\[243\]](#page-38-9) and [Minot.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minot,_North_Dakota)[\[244\]](#page-38-10)

In [Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio) there was a rally in [Cleveland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleveland) in [Public Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Square,_Cleveland) followed by a march through the streets of downtown. [\[245\]](#page-39-0)[\[246\]](#page-39-1) In [Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati) a rally took place at City Hall, which followed a performance with seventeen flutes made from shotgun barrels, as a memorial to the victims of the Parkland shooting.[\[247\]](#page-39-2) Another march occurred in [Columbus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbus,_Ohio) at the [Ohio Statehouse,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_Statehouse)[\[248\]](#page-39-3) and other rallies took place in [Athens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athens,_Ohio) and [Dayton,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dayton,_Ohio) each drawing hundreds of protesters.

#### **South**

In [Alabama,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alabama) marches took place in [Birmingham,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birmingham,_Alabama) [Mobile,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobile,_Alabama) [Dothan,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dothan,_Alabama) [Montgomery,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montgomery,_Alabama) [Selma,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selma,_Alabama) [Jasper,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jasper,_Alabama) and [Florence.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florence,_Alabama) The largest of these, the Birmingham march, drew over 5,000 attendees.[\[258\]](#page-40-1)

In [Florida,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florida) demonstrations took place in [Gulf Breeze,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_Breeze,_Florida)[\[259\]](#page-40-2) [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Beach) [Beach,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Beach) [Orlando](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orlando,_Florida)[\[260\]](#page-40-3) and [West Palm Beach,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Palm_Beach)[\[261\]](#page-40-4) Naples where 3,000 people gathered at [Cambier Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Cambier_Park&action=edit&redlink=1) for the march and a rally. The Orlando march was organized by students of UCF, and was held at [Lake Eola Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Eola_Park) in [downtown Orlando](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Orlando) with 35,000 people in attendance[.\[260\]](#page-40-3) The city where the school shooting occurred, [Parkland,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parkland,_Florida) also had a march.[\[262\]](#page-40-5) Similar marches were also held in northeast Florida in [Jacksonville,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacksonville) [Fernandina Beach,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernandina_Beach) and [Saint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Augustine) [Augustine.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Augustine)[\[263\]](#page-40-6)

A rally in [Jacksonville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacksonville,_Florida) had a turnout of over 1,000 people in Hemming Park. Hundreds of people marched from Hemming Park to the [Duval County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duval_County,_Florida) Courthouse with signs including: "Grab them by the mid-terms" and ["#neveragain"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_for_Our_Lives#neveragain).[\[264\]](#page-40-7) Notable speakers included John Phillips, the civil attorney of Jordan Davis, the seventeen year old who was shot and killed at a gas station in 2012 and *[Stranger Things](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stranger_Things)* actor Chester Rushing. In [Saint Augustine,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Augustine) the march began by walking over the [Bridge of](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bridge_of_Lions) [Lions,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bridge_of_Lions) down Avenida Menenedez, ending at Fort Castillo. In the state of [Washington,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_(state)) marches attended by hundreds took place in [Spokane,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spokane,_Washington)[\[344\]](#page-48-6) and [Yakima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yakima)[.\[345\]](#page-48-7) Thousands marched in [Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle) and [Bellingham.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellingham,_Washington)[\[346\]](#page-48-8)[\[347\]](#page-49-0)

#### **Puerto Rico**

In an official announcement to the state, Governor [Ricardo Rosselló](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ricardo_Rossell%C3%B3) announced that he commissioned the [Secretary of State,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_State_of_Puerto_Rico) [Luis G. Rivera Marín,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_G._Rivera_Mar%C3%ADn) to begin preparations for the march in [San Juan.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Juan,_Puerto_Rico)[\[348\]](#page-49-1) Rosselló called for all citizens and civic, religious, and private sector organizations to stand united in solidarity for improved gun control.[\[348\]](#page-49-1) He also remarked that [Puerto Rico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerto_Rico) has the strictest gun control regulations of all jurisdictions in the country.[\[348\]](#page-49-1) Rivera Marín stated that "our communities need to be a place where our people have peace, not fear".[\[349\]](#page-49-2) He announced that the march would begin at [Condado Lagoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Condado_Lagoon) and culminate at the Peace Pavilion in [Luis Muñoz Rivera Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Mu%C3%B1oz_Rivera_Park)[.\[350\]](#page-49-3)

At the end of November 2020, [Kemuel Delgado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kemuel_Delgado) established the first March for Our Lives chapter in Puerto Rico.[\[351\]](#page-49-4)[\[352\]](#page-49-5)

### **Outside the United States**

#### **North America**

#### **Map**

Throughout Canada, in the province of British Columbia, marches were planned in [Vancouver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vancouver) and [Victoria.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria,_British_Columbia) [\[353\]](#page-49-6)

Marches were set to take place in [Calgary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calgary) and [Edmonton,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmonton) Alberta.[\[353\]](#page-49-6)

Students from [R. D. Parker Collegiate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R._D._Parker_Collegiate) in [Thompson,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thompson,_Manitoba) Manitoba, were planning to march.[\[353\]](#page-49-6)

In Ontario, marches were planned in [Ottawa,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottawa)[\[354\]](#page-49-7) [Guelph,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guelph) [Kitchener,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kitchener,_Ontario)[\[353\]](#page-49-6) [Stratford,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stratford,_Ontario)[\[355\]](#page-50-0) [Toronto,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto)[\[356\]](#page-50-1) and [Waterloo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterloo,_Ontario)[.\[357\]](#page-50-2)

In Quebec marches were planned in [Montreal,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montreal) [Westmount,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westmount,_Quebec)[\[358\]](#page-50-3) [Quebec City,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_City) and [Sherbrooke.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sherbrooke)[\[353\]](#page-49-6)

New Brunswick was set to have a march in [Fredericton.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fredericton)[\[353\]](#page-49-6)

Newfoundland and Labrador planned a march to take place in [St. John's.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._John%27s,_Newfoundland_and_Labrador)[\[353\]](#page-49-6)

#### **Africa**

Marches were planned in [Cairo,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairo) Egypt, [Pretoria,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pretoria) [Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town) and [Johannesburg,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johannesburg) South Africa, [Accra,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accra) Ghana; [\[359\]](#page-50-4) and Mozambique[.\[360\]](#page-50-5)

#### **Asia**

#### **Map**

Survivors from the Parkland shooting spoke at rallies in [Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem) and [Tel Aviv,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_Aviv) Israel. The event in Tel Aviv took place at the country's U. S. Embassy.[\[361\]](#page-50-6)

Marches were planned for [Shanghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanghai) and [Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong) China, [Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai) and [New Delhi,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Delhi) [Tokyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tokyo) and [Okinawa,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Okinawa_Prefecture) [Mingora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mingora) [\(Malala Yousafzai'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malala_Yousafzai)s hometown), [Karachi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karachi) and [Islamabad,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamabad) Pakistan as well as [Kabul,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabul) [Jakarta,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jakarta) [Kuala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuala_Lumpur) [Lumpur,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuala_Lumpur) [Abu Dhabi,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi) [Dubai,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai) and [Manila.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manila)[\[354\]](#page-49-7)

#### **Europe**

#### **Map**

Students from the [International School of Geneva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_School_of_Geneva) in Switzerland organized a rally outside the European headquarters of the United Nations, attracting hundreds of students[.\[362\]](#page-50-7)

In Germany, protests were held in [Berlin,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berlin)[\[363\]](#page-51-0) [Hamburg,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamburg)[\[363\]](#page-51-0) [Frankfurt,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankfurt)[\[364\]](#page-51-1) [Friedrichshafen,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friedrichshafen) [Heidelberg,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heidelberg) [Munich,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich) and [Wiesbaden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wiesbaden)[.\[365\]](#page-51-2) Events also took place in [Reykjavík](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reykjav%C3%ADk)[,\[366\]](#page-51-3) [Barcelona,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barcelona)[\[367\]](#page-51-4) [Oslo,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo)[\[367\]](#page-51-4) [Stockholm,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stockholm)[\[364\]](#page-51-1) and [Geneva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva)[.\[362\]](#page-50-7)

In the United Kingdom, hundreds marched outside the [US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_the_United_States,_London) [embassy in London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_the_United_States,_London)[.\[368\]](#page-51-5) Marchers also held a "die in" and lay on

## ![](_page_13_Picture_19.jpeg)

Support for "March For Our Lives" in [Museumplein, Amsterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museumplein)

the ground outside the US embassy to show solidarity with the [Parkland students.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Never_Again_MSD)[\[369\]](#page-51-6) Demonstrations also took place in [Belfast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belfast)[\[370\]](#page-51-7) and [London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London)[.\[371\]](#page-52-0) In Scotland, relatives of the [Dunblane massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunblane_massacre) victims joined a demonstration outside the US consulate in [Edinburgh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edinburgh)[.\[372\]](#page-52-1)

International and American students rallied in [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rome) near the [US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_embassy) [embassy,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_embassy) some holding signs that read "Protect People, Not Guns", "Enough is Enough", and "Dress Codes Are More Regulated Than Guns", as they wanted to make their voice clear to America.[\[373\]](#page-52-2) The march in Rome was organized by the Rome chapter of American Expats for Positive Change (AEPC); the organizers stated their efforts to support America and safety in U. S. schools, even though gun violence was not solely an American issue.[\[374\]](#page-52-3)

In [Amsterdam,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amsterdam) hundreds participated in a demonstration near the US consulate on the [Museumplein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museumplein)[.\[375\]](#page-52-4) The organizers and speakers included American and Dutch high school students, an

## ![](_page_14_Picture_4.jpeg)

Support for "March For Our Lives" in [Geneva, Switzerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva)

alumna of Marjory Stoneman Douglas High School, and a Florida student walkout organizer who lost a friend in the Parkland shooting[.\[376\]](#page-52-5)[\[375\]](#page-52-4)[\[377\]](#page-52-6)

Protests were also held in [Vienna,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vienna) [Paris,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris) [The Hague,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hague) [Helsinki,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helsinki) [Budapest,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budapest) [Bucharest,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bucharest) [So](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sofia)fia, [Riga,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riga) [Málaga,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%A1laga) [Lisbon,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lisbon) [Warsaw,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw) [Minsk,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minsk)[\[376\]](#page-52-5) [Majorca,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majorca) [Copenhagen,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copenhagen)[\[378\]](#page-52-7) and [Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels)[.\[379\]](#page-52-8)[\[371\]](#page-52-0)[\[380\]](#page-52-9)

#### **Oceania**

#### **Map**

Marches were planned for [Melbourne,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melbourne) [Brisbane,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brisbane) [Canberra,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canberra)[\[381\]](#page-52-10) and [Sydney,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney) Australia[.\[382\]](#page-52-11)

New Zealand planned marches in [Albert Park,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Park,_Auckland) [Auckland;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auckland) [Parliament House, Wellington;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_House,_Wellington) [Cathedral Square,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_Square,_Christchurch) [Christchurch;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_Square,_Christchurch) and Union Hall at the [University of Otago,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Otago) [Dunedin.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunedin)[\[383\]](#page-53-0)

#### **South America**

In Argentina, a march was planned in [Buenos Aires.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buenos_Aires)[\[354\]](#page-49-7)

In Brazil, two marches was planned in [Rio de Janeiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_de_Janeiro) and [São Paulo.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo)

Other protests took place in [Lima,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lima) Peru, [Bogotá,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bogot%C3%A1) Colombia, [Georgetown,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgetown,_Guyana) Guyana and [Caracas,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caracas) Venezuela, the city where Parkland victim [Joaquin Oliver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joaquin_Oliver) and his family escaped from and immigrated to the U. S. to live a better life because of the city's violence and poverty. They officially became American citizens last year at the time in November 2017.
